<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01NcRL5yxSp151u69FopGV4u`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Measurement error in causal inference.}
5	The statistical foundations are established in \citet{carroll2006} and \citet{gustafson2004}, with starred-node DAGs formalized by \citet{hernan_cole2009}. For binary variables, non-differential misclassification biases toward the null~\cite{brenner1993, ogburn_vanderweele2012}, but \citet{dosemeci1990} and \citet{jurek2005} showed this fails for polytomous exposures. \citet{veirod_laake2001} derived multi-category bias expressions for Poisson regression, and \citet{edwards_lessler_naimi2021} showed dependent misclassification can bias in either direction. All of these works analyze a single DAG topology; our taxonomy is the first to characterize how the \emph{same} confusion matrix produces qualitatively different bias across seven topologies.
6	
7	\paragraph{Text classifiers and LLM annotation.}
8	\citet{grimmer2022} provide the foundation for text-as-data, while \citet{wood_doughty2018} first applied DAG reasoning to classifier error. \citet{teblunthuis2024} gave the most complete binary treatment (four DAG scenarios, $K{=}2$), and \citet{lee_wood_doughty2024} extended to categorical LLM classifiers but covered only confounding. On annotation quality, \citet{baumann2025} found 31\% incorrect conclusions with LLM labels and \citet{kim_garg2025} showed 60\% error correlation across models. These works either restrict to binary labels, cover a single topology, or diagnose quality without connecting it to causal validity. Our framework provides the missing link.
9	
10	\paragraph{Sensitivity analysis and correction methods.}
11	Our ASV framework draws on E-values for unmeasured confounding~\cite{vanderweele_ding2017} and probabilistic bias analysis~\cite{lash2009}. Unlike E-values, ASVs comprise three types (magnitude, significance, sign-flip) because failure modes are topology-dependent; unlike PBA, ASVs provide monotonicity guarantees over $K{\times}K$ matrices. For correction, PPI++~\cite{angelopoulos2023} and DSL~\cite{egami_hinck_stewart_wei2023} are designed as topology-agnostic. Our contribution reveals that this agnosticism is costly: correction effectiveness is itself topology-dependent, with matrix-based methods provably ineffective for four topologies.
12	
```

</details>

    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\paragraph{Measurement error in causal inference.}\nThe statistical foundations are established in \\citet{carroll2006} and \\citet{gustafson2004}, with starred-node DAGs formalized by \\citet{hernan_cole2009}. For binary variables, non-differential misclassification biases toward the null~\\cite{brenner1993, ogburn_vanderweele2012}, but \\citet{dosemeci1990} and \\citet{jurek2005} showed this fails for polytomous exposures. \\citet{veirod_laake2001} derived multi-category bias expressions for Poisson regression, and \\citet{edwards_lessler_naimi2021} showed dependent misclassification can bias in either direction. All of these works analyze a single DAG topology; our taxonomy is the first to characterize how the \\emph{same} confusion matrix produces qualitatively different bias across seven topologies.\n\n\\paragraph{Text classifiers and LLM annotation.}\n\\citet{grimmer2022} provide the foundation for text-as-data, while \\citet{wood_doughty2018} first applied DAG reasoning to classifier error. \\citet{teblunthuis2024} gave the most complete binary treatment (four DAG scenarios, $K{=}2$), and \\citet{lee_wood_doughty2024} extended to categorical LLM classifiers but covered only confounding. <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01VqQM4MGMLkYdcUVRmFMBcz`</summary>

```text
105	For four topologies where $A$ enters as a covariate (confounding, mediation, collider, M-bias), the Frisch--Waugh--Lovell theorem implies that applying $C^{-1}$ to the dummy variables cannot change the $T$ coefficient. Naive plug-in and MLA achieve exactly 0.0\% bias reduction across these four topologies.
106	
107	\paragraph{Method ranking.}
108	Measuring RMSE reduction ($1 - \text{RMSE}_{\text{corr}} / \text{RMSE}_{\text{uncorr}}$), PPI++ achieves the highest grand-mean reduction (47.0\%) but requires gold labels and is catastrophically harmful for M-bias ($-$88.0\%). MC-SIMEX provides gold-free correction (13.3\% grand mean, best for IV at 59.4\%) but harms front-door ($-$62.4\%). DSL corrects confounding (48.0\%) and mediation (52.9\%) but amplifies M-bias ($-$267\%) and IV ($-$206\%). No single method dominates: correction effectiveness is itself topology-dependent.
109	
110	Empirical validation on HumAID ($K{=}10$, 30 MC replicates) confirms topology-dependent method superiority: MC-SIMEX outperforms PPI++ in 4 of 7 topologies and reverses from harmful ($K{=}3$) to beneficial ($K{=}10$). A retrospective case study applying the full framework to \citet{lee_wood_doughty2024} is in Appendix~\ref{app:case_study}.
111	
```

```text
70	
71	\subsection{Annotation Sensitivity Values}
72	\label{sec:asv}
73	
74	We introduce Annotation Sensitivity Values (ASVs) as a unified sensitivity analysis framework, analogous to E-values for unmeasured confounding~\cite{vanderweele_ding2017} but designed for annotation-induced bias.
75	
76	\paragraph{Parameterization.}
77	We parameterize the confusion matrix as $C(\delta) = (1{-}\delta)I + \delta C_0$, where $C_0$ is a reference confusion matrix (e.g., estimated from a validation set) and $\delta \geq 0$ controls the severity of misclassification. At $\delta{=}0$, classification is perfect; at $\delta{=}1$, classification matches $C_0$. Non-negativity of $C(\delta)$ requires $\delta \leq \delta_{\max} = \min_j \frac{1}{1 - (C_0)_{jj}}$. Since $(C_0)_{jj} \in [0,1]$, we have $\delta_{\max} \geq 1$ for all valid $C_0$, ensuring the full range $[0,1]$ is always feasible; all ASV computations are constrained to $[0, \delta_{\max}]$.
78	
79	\paragraph{Three ASV types.}
80	We define three complementary measures: (1)~\textbf{Magnitude ASV} $\delta^*_{\text{mag}} = \min\{\delta : |\text{plim}\;\hat{\tau}(C(\delta)) - \tau| > \epsilon\}$---analogous to E-values; (2)~\textbf{Significance ASV} $\delta^*_{\text{sig}} = \min\{\delta : |\text{plim}\;\hat{\tau}(C(\delta))| < z_{\alpha/2} \cdot SE(N)\}$---relevant for attenuating topologies; (3)~\textbf{Sign-flip ASV} $\delta^*_{\text{sf}} = \min\{\delta : \text{sign}(\hat{\tau}) \neq \text{sign}(\tau)\}$---exposure and IV only. The significance ASV depends on $N$ through $SE(N)$, exhibiting a sharp phase transition at a critical $N^*$ below which all rejections are blocked.
81	
82	\begin{theorem}[Envelope monotonicity---confounding]
83	\label{thm:monotone}
84	For the confounding topology under $C(\delta) = (1{-}\delta)I + \delta C_0$, $|B(C(\delta))|$ is monotonically non-decreasing in $\delta \in [0, \delta_{\max}]$.
85	\end{theorem}
86	
87	\emph{Proof sketch.} When $f {=} \lambda g$ (proportional effects), $B(C(\delta)) {=} \lambda V_W(\delta)/(V_W(\delta) {+} \sigma_T^2)$ where $V_W(\delta) {=} \operatorname{Var}(g) {-} \operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g{\mid}A^*_\delta])$. As $\delta$ increases, $A^*$ becomes a noisier channel for $A$, so $\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g{\mid}A^*_\delta])$ decreases by the data processing inequality, increasing $V_W$. Since $x/(x{+}c)$ is increasing for $c{>}0$, $|B|$ is non-decreasing. The general case follows from Cauchy--Schwarz bounds on $R(C(\delta))$ (Appendix~\ref{app:confounding}).
88	
89	\begin{conjecture}[Envelope monotonicity---remaining topologies]
```

At $\\delta{=}0$, classification is perfect; at $\\delta{=}1$, classification matches $C_0$. Non-negativity of $C(\\delta)$ requires $\\delta \\leq \\delta_{\\max} = \\min_j \\frac{1}{1 - (C_0)_{jj}}$. Since $(C_0)_{jj} \\in [0,1]$, we have $\\delta_{\\max} \\geq 1$ for all valid $C_0$, ensuring the full range $[0,1]$ is always feasible; all ASV computations are constrained to $[0, \\delta_{\\max}]$.\n\n\\paragraph{Three ASV types.}\nWe define three complementary measures: (1)~\\textbf{Magnitude ASV} $\\delta^*_{\\text{mag}} = \\min\\{\\delta : |\\text{plim}\\;\\hat{\\tau}(C(\\delta)) - \\tau| > \\epsilon\\}$---analogous to E-values; (2)~\\textbf{Significance ASV} $\\delta^*_{\\text{sig}} = \\min\\{\\delta : |\\text{plim}\\;\\hat{\\tau}(C(\\delta))| < z_{\\alpha/2} \\cdot SE(N)\\}$---relevant for attenuating topologies; (3)~\\textbf{Sign-flip ASV} $\\delta^*_{\\text{sf}} = \\min\\{\\delta : \\text{sign}(\\hat{\\tau}) \\neq \\text{sign}(\\tau)\\}$---exposure and IV only. The significance ASV depends on $N$ through $SE(N)$, exhibiting a sharp phase transition at a critical $N^*$ below which all rejections are blocked.\n\n\\begin{theorem}[Envelope monotonicity---confounding]\n\\label{thm:monotone}\nFor the confounding topology under $C(\\delta) = (1{-}\\delta)I + \\delta C_0$, $|B(C(\\delta))|$ is monotonically non-decreasing in $\\delta \\in [0, \\delta_{\\max}]$.\n\\end{theorem}\n\n\\emph{Proof sketch.} When $f {=} \\lambda g$ (proportional effects), $B(C(\\delta)) {=} \\lambda V_W(\\delta)/(V_W(\\delta) {+} \\sigma_T^2)$ where $V_W(\\delta) {=} \\operatorname{Var}(g) {-} \\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g{\\mid}A^*_\\delta])$. As $\\delta$ increases, $A^*$ becomes a noisier channel for $A$, so $\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g{\\mid}A^*_\\delta])$ decreases by the data processing inequality, increasing $V_W$. Since $x/(x{+}c)$ is increasing for $c{>}0$, $|B|$ is non-decreasing. The general case follows from Cauchy--Schwarz bounds on $R(C(\\delta))$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:confounding}).\n\n\\begin{conjecture}[Envelope monotonicity---remaining topologies]",
      "numLines": 20,
      "startLine": 70,
      "totalLines": 98
    }
  },

{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "1378960f-2217-41de-92ae-086d9856d475",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "4d58ff4e-d19d-4e80-8c6c-2131e935df92",

  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-prod_0510/projects/causal_annot_error_attrib/docs/paper/conclusion.tex",
    "oldString": "VAST and CivilComments use a 7B model rather than frontier models, limiting cross-tier comparisons.",
    "newString": "VAST and CivilComments use a 7B model rather than frontier models, limiting cross-tier comparisons. All derivations assume linear structural equations; the FWL invariance result does not hold for logistic regression, and bias patterns under nonlinear models may differ.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe presented a systematic characterization of how multi-class annotation errors interact with causal DAG topology, jointly analyzing bias direction, sensitivity, and correction across seven canonical DAG topologies. The classical binary guarantee breaks for $K \\geq 3$: exposure and IV topologies produce sign reversal (83.4\\% and 52.6\\%), while five topologies maintain a structural guarantee. The ASV framework reveals a vulnerability spectrum from IV (maximally fragile) to M-bias (immune), and correction effectiveness is itself topology-dependent, with matrix-based methods provably ineffective for four topologies. Immediate extensions include differential misclassification and correlated multi-LLM error structures.\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur framework assumes non-differential misclassification ($P(A^*|A, Y, \\mathbf{Z}) = P(A^*|A)$), which holds when the LLM has no access to $Y$ but may be violated with outcome-conditional prompting. A stress test (Appendix~\\ref{sec:nondiff_stress}) shows moderate robustness ($\\varepsilon{=}0.05$ increases violations by $<$3pp). Closed-form expressions are derived for $K{=}3$ and verified at $K{=}5,7$; arbitrary $K$ remains open. The framework assumes a single annotator; correlated multi-annotator settings~\\cite{kim_garg2025} are future work. VAST and CivilComments use a 7B model rather than frontier models, limiting cross-tier comparisons.\n",

78	\paragraph{HumAID ($K{=}10$): theory--practice alignment.}
79	The empirical bias ranking matches predictions: IV (12.4--42.1\%), exposure (9.0--17.4\%), mediation/front-door (10.7--14.8\%), while collider, confounding, and M-bias remain below 10\%---consistent with the ASV vulnerability spectrum.
80	
81	\paragraph{Cross-$K$ comparison.}
82	Bias varies substantially across settings: VAST ($K{=}3$) produces 65.4\% mean bias with IV reaching 252.6\%, versus 12.2\% for HumAID ($K{=}10$) and 37.0\% for CivilComments ($K{=}2$). Even binary tasks with $F_1{=}0.72$--$0.75$ produce catastrophic IV bias (101--131\%), confirming that classification accuracy does not predict downstream causal risk (Figure~\ref{fig:f1_vs_bias}).\footnote{VAST and CivilComments use a 7B model (vs.\ frontier for HumAID), and VAST's near-degenerate confusion matrix (third-class recall $=4.6\%$) may drive its extreme IV bias. These factors limit cross-dataset comparisons but not within-dataset topology-dominance conclusions.}
83	
84	Zero sign reversals occur across all 98 bias scenarios ($14 \times 7$), confirming the structural sign-flip guarantee is $K$-invariant.\footnote{Two additional frontier-model configurations for VAST extend this to 112 scenarios across 16 configurations.} The 7B-to-235B bias ratio is 1.24, indicating topology rather than model scale determines downstream bias.
85	
86	\paragraph{ASV diagnostic results.}
87	For HumAID ($K{=}10$, 56 scenarios), 55.4\% are flagged as magnitude-ASV-unsafe---IV, mediation, front-door, and exposure all trigger, while confounding, collider, and M-bias remain below 10\% bias (Figure~\ref{fig:asv_heatmap}). For CivilComments ($K{=}2$), 100\% of scenarios are ASV-unsafe despite $F_1{=}0.72$--$0.75$.
88	
89	Overlaying all 18 empirical matrices onto the theoretical Dirichlet violation-rate curves (Figure~\ref{fig:dirichlet_overlay}, Appendix~\ref{app:supplementary}) confirms placement: VAST in the danger zone, HumAID mid-range, CivilComments approaching safe for exposure but still in IV amplification.
90	
91	\begin{figure}[t]
92	\centering
```

12	\label{sec:taxonomy_results}
13	
14	\paragraph{Theory-MC agreement.}
15	Analytical probability limits match Monte Carlo estimates with grand MAPE of 2.0\% across 126 scenarios (18 LLM configurations $\times$ 7 topologies, $N{=}10{,}000$). Per-topology MAPE ranges from 0.5\% (confounding) to 5.5\% (IV); the IV maximum reflects the Wald estimator's small-denominator variance amplification.
16	
17	\paragraph{Exposure DAG: catastrophic sign reversal.}
18	Under the full Dirichlet$(1,1,1)$ prior, 85\% of $K{=}3$ confusion matrices produce bias that violates the binary ``toward-null'' guarantee. More critically, 83.4\% of matrices cause \emph{sign reversal} of at least one coefficient. The two failure modes are distinct: for a coefficient with true value $\beta_1{=}1.0$, a near-permutation $C$ produces $\hat{\gamma}_1{=}{-}0.84$ (sign reversal, magnitude $0.84{\times}$); for $\beta_2{=}{-}0.5$, the same $C$ yields $\hat{\gamma}_2{=}{-}1.41$ (same-sign amplification, $2.83{\times}$).
19

  "old_string": "\\begin{algorithm}[h]\n\\caption{Correction method selection for LLM-annotated causal inference.}\n\\label{alg:correction}",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Error structures used in correction experiments.}\nThe six confusion matrix structures used for Table~\\ref{tab:correction} are:\n(1)~\\emph{Diagonal-dominant}: $C_{kk}{=}0.7$, off-diagonal uniform;\n(2)~\\emph{Moderate noise}: $C_{kk}{=}0.5$, off-diagonal uniform;\n(3)~\\emph{Near-permutation}: $C_{kk}{=}0.4$, largest off-diagonal 0.5;\n(4)~\\emph{Asymmetric}: $C_{kk}$ varies (0.8, 0.5, 0.3);\n(5)~\\emph{Block confusion}: two classes confused ($C_{12}{=}C_{21}{=}0.4$), third clean;\n(6)~\\emph{Heavy noise}: $C_{kk}{=}0.4$, off-diagonal uniform.\nAll are column-stochastic with $K{=}3$. Results are averaged across all six structures.\n\n\\begin{algorithm}[h]\n\\caption{Correction method selection for LLM-annotated causal inference.}\n\\label{alg:correction}"
}

(immune), and correction effectiveness is itself

624

630

Our framework assumes non-differential misclassification (P (A∗ |A, Y, Z) = P (A∗ |A)),
which holds when the LLM has no access to Y
but may be violated with outcome-conditional
prompting. A stress test (Appendix B.3)
shows moderate robustness (ε=0.05 increases
violations by <3pp). Closed-form expressions
are derived for K=3 and verified at K=5, 7;
arbitrary K remains open. The framework

[Director 指令] 用户确认 API key 可用，要求全力冲刺 4.0。以下任务按优先级执行：

## 任务 1: plan011 frontier annotations（最高优先级）

用 `openrouter_api_key`（通过 request_secret 获取）测试 API 连通性：
```
curl -H "Authorization: [REDACTED]" http://47.94.22.126/v1/models
```

如果连通，立即运行 plan011：用 frontier 模型（Qwen3-235B, Llama-4-Maverick, DeepSeek-Chat）标注 VAST(K=3) 和 CivilComments(K=2)，生成 confusion matrices。这直接解决审稿意见 W8（模型不对称：HumAID 用 frontier 但 VAST/CC 用 7B）。

如果 API 仍然 401，立即报告，不要反复重试。

同时检查 plan003 剩余配置 qwen3_fs5 是否也能补上。

## 任务 2: 论文额外加强（与任务 1 并行，在本地完成）

以下修改直接在论文 .tex 文件中进行：

### 2a. Table 1 collider 表述矛盾澄清
审稿人指出 Table 1 Collider 显示 Violation=100%、Amplification=100%，但正文称 "protective"。在 Table 1 caption 或 experiments.tex 中加一句解释：violation 和 amplification 是相对于 τ（真值），而 "protective" 是相对于 perfect-classification collider bias B(I)——misclassification 减弱了 collider stratification 引入的 spurious association。两个比较基准不同。

### 2b. Cross-PPI gold-free 讨论深化
在 experiments.tex Section 4.6 correction guidance 部分，扩展 Cross-PPI (Zrnic & Candès 2024) 讨论：
- Cross-PPI 不需要 gold labels，只需两个独立的 LLM annotators
- 这绕过了 PPI++ 的 gold label 要求
- 但 Cross-PPI 的 bias reduction 依赖两个 annotators 的误差独立性假设
- 在我们的框架下，Cross-PPI 可视为 topology-agnostic 的 correction（不受 FWL invariance 限制，因为它不是 matrix-based）
这回应了审稿人 Q3 "是否存在绕过 FWL invariance 的 correction 方法"。

### 2c. Novelty 论证强化
在 introduction.tex 中，找到讨论 K≥3 质变的段落，加强以下论点：
- Binary (K=2) 的 sign-flip impossibility 是 2×2 column-stochastic matrix 的代数性质（正对角线 → 特征值 > 0），这个证明在 K≥3 结构上就不成立
- 这不是"多测了几种情况"的增量，而是发现了一个 qualitatively new failure mode
- 引用 Brenner 1993 只提到了 polytomous misclassification 的可能性但未量化跨拓扑差异

注意：所有修改必须保持 main text ≤ 8 页。如果空间不够，压缩其他段落。

## 任务 3: 如果 plan011 成功，更新论文数据

plan011 成功后：
- 更新 experiments.tex 中的实证验证部分，补充 VAST/CC 的 frontier model 结果
- 更新 abstract/intro 中的 scenario count（从 98 增加）
- 确认 zero sign flips 是否仍然成立

完成后汇报每个任务的状态。

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:

   - Phase 1: Compute δ_max constraint for ASV parameterization (C(δ) non-negativity)
   - Phase 2: Split Proposition 1 into proved+empirical, add explicit conditions, prove Theorem 1 for confounding, downgrade rest to Conjecture
   - Phase 3: Calibrate 83.4% headline with dual regime (full prior + realistic accuracy), weaken "first" claims
   - Phase 4: Restore 4 citations removed during page fitting (audinet2025, zrnic_candes2024, gligoric2025, keith2020)
   - Phase 5: Multiple should-fix items (bootstrap C₀, linearity limitation, zero sign-flip explanation, error structure descriptions, table caption fixes)
   - Hard constraint: main text ≤ 8 pages throughout all changes
   
   **Second instruction (latest, NOT YET EXECUTED)**: Three tasks:
   - Task 1 (highest priority): Test API connectivity via `request_secret` for `openrouter_api_key`, then run plan011 frontier annotations for VAST(K=3) and CivilComments(K=2) with Qwen3-235B/Llama-4-Maverick/DeepSeek-Chat. Also check plan003 qwen3_fs5.
   - Task 2 (parallel with Task 1): Three paper modifications: (a) Clarify Table 1 collider "protective" vs Violation=100% apparent contradiction, (b) Deepen Cross-PPI gold-free discussion in correction section, (c) Strengthen novelty argument in introduction about K≥3 qualitative break
   - Task 3 (after Task 1 success): Update paper with new frontier annotation data

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - ASV (Annotation Sensitivity Values) parameterization: C(δ) = (1-δ)I + δC₀, with δ_max = min_j 1/(1-(C₀)_{jj})
   - δ_max ≥ 1 for all valid C₀ (mathematical guarantee since diagonal entries ∈ [0,1])
   - Data processing inequality for monotonicity proof: noisier channel → less variance in conditional expectation
   - Proposition split: analytic proofs (confounding Cauchy-Schwarz, collider Berkson attenuation) vs empirical findings (mediation, M-bias, front-door; 100k matrix verification)
   - Theorem 1 restricted to confounding; Conjecture 1 for remaining 6 topologies
   - FWL invariance: matrix-based corrections achieve 0% bias reduction for 4 covariate topologies
   - Cross-PPI (Zrnić & Candès 2024): gold-free PPI variant using two independent annotators
   - EMNLP 2026 format: 8pp main text, unlimited references, Limitations/Ethics don't count
   - ACL template: `\usepackage[review]{acl}`, 11pt two-column

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`docs/paper/method.tex`** (heavily modified across Phases 1, 2, 5)
     - Phase 1: Added δ_max constraint to ASV parameterization paragraph (line 77)
     - Phase 2: Split Proposition into Proposition 1 (proved) + Empirical Finding 1 (lines 57-69); Replaced Theorem 1 with confounding-specific theorem + proof sketch + Conjecture 1 (lines 82-95)
     - Phase 5: Added bootstrap C₀ recommendation after parameterization
     - Key current state of Proposition block:
       ```latex
       \begin{proposition}[Sign preservation---analytic results]
       \label{prop:signflip}
       Under non-differential misclassification with $C_{kk} > 0$ for all $k$:
       \emph{(a)}~Confounding preserves sign when $\operatorname{sign}(\tau) = \operatorname{sign}(\tau {+} B_{\mathrm{OV}})$...
       \emph{(b)}~Collider preserves sign when $|\tau| > \sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g)\operatorname{Var}(h)}/\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T{\mid}A)]$...
       \emph{(c)}~Exposure can produce sign reversal for $K {\geq} 3$...
       \end{proposition}
       
       \paragraph{Empirical Finding 1} (Mediation, M-bias, front-door).
       Sign preservation holds under topology-specific conditions...
       
       These conditions involve true DGP parameters... The ASV framework provides a complementary assessment...
       ```

   - **`docs/paper/experiments.tex`** (heavily modified across page fitting + Phases 4, 5)
     - Page fitting: Massive compression of Cross-K, Caveat→footnote, ASV diagnostic, Method ranking, removed Empirical correction validation and Applied illustration paragraphs (merged to 2 sentences), compressed Setup/Theory-MC/HumAID, N-Sensitivity→paragraph, Figure captions
     - Phase 4: Added Cross-PPI and FWL bypass methods in correction section
     - Phase 5: Added zero sign-flip consistency explanation, improved Table 1 caption
     - Key addition in correction section: "Gold-free PPI variants~\cite{zrnic_candes2024} may relax the gold-label requirement; methods bypassing FWL---such as EM imputation of latent true labels and Bayesian measurement error models---offer potential alternatives for the four FWL-invariant topologies."

        "content": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Setup and Notation}\n\\label{sec:notation}\n\nLet $A$ denote a true $K$-class categorical variable (e.g., sentiment with $K{=}3$ levels) and $A^*$ its observed, potentially misclassified version produced by an LLM annotator. The misclassification process is characterized by a $K{\\times}K$ confusion matrix $C$ with entries $C_{jk} = P(A^*{=}j \\mid A{=}k)$, where each column sums to one. Following \\citet{hernan_cole2009}, we represent this measurement process using \\emph{starred-node} DAGs: the true variable $A$ is latent, and the observed $A^*$ is connected to $A$ via a dashed measurement edge.\n\n\\begin{definition}[Non-differential misclassification]\n\\label{def:nondiff}\nMisclassification of $A$ is non-differential with respect to outcome $Y$ and covariates $\\mathbf{Z}$ if $P(A^*{=}j \\mid A{=}k, Y, \\mathbf{Z}) = P(A^*{=}j \\mid A{=}k) = C_{jk}$ for all $j,k$.\n\\end{definition}\n\nWe study seven canonical DAG topologies (Figure~\\ref{fig:dag_taxonomy})~\\cite{pearl2009} in which $A$ occupies structurally distinct causal roles: confounder, exposure, mediator, collider, M-bias variable, front-door mediator, and instrument. Each role induces a different functional relationship between $C$ and the probability limit of the standard causal estimator, yielding topology-dependent bias.\n\n\\subsection{Multi-Class Bias Expressions}\n\\label{sec:bias_expressions}\n\nFor each topology, we derive the probability limit of the naive estimator that substitutes $A^*$ for $A$. Let $\\tau$ denote the target causal parameter and $\\hat{\\tau}(A^*)$ the estimator using misclassified annotations.\n\n\\paragraph{Confounding ($A$ = confounder).}\nWhen $A$ confounds the $X{\\to}Y$ relationship, the OLS estimator controlling for $A^*$ has probability limit\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:confounding}\n\\text{plim}\\;\\hat{\\tau}(A^*) = \\bigl(\\mathbb{E}[Z^{\\prime}Z]\\bigr)^{-1}\\mathbb{E}[Z^{\\prime}X]\\,\\boldsymbol{\\beta},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $Z = [X, \\text{dummies}(A^*)]$, $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ contains the true coefficients, and the target parameter $\\tau$ is the coefficient on $X$. Misclassification produces residual confounding that monotonically approaches the omitted variable bias, but the sign of $\\hat{\\tau}$ is preserved: $\\tau_{\\text{sign\\_flip}} = 0$ structurally.\\footnote{This result is consistent with \\citet{brenner1993}, who showed that the adjusted estimate with misclassified polytomous confounders can be biased toward or away from the null. Both findings hold simultaneously: misclassification degrades confounding control, producing residual bias whose magnitude can exceed the correctly adjusted estimate (amplification), but the treatment effect sign is never reversed. The distinction is between \\emph{sign reversal} ($\\text{sign}(\\hat{\\tau}) \\neq \\text{sign}(\\tau)$, which we show never occurs) and \\emph{amplification} ($|\\hat{\\tau}| > |\\tau|$, which occurs universally in this topology).}\n\n\\paragraph{Exposure ($A$ = treatment variable).}\nWhen $A$ is the exposure of interest, the biased coefficient for category $j$ becomes a weighted combination of true coefficients:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:exposure}\n\\text{plim}\\;\\hat{\\beta}_j(A^*) = \\sum_{k=1}^{K} w_{jk}\\,\\beta_k,\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $w_{jk}$ depends on the confusion matrix $C$ and the marginal distribution of $A$. Unlike the binary case, where non-differential misclassification guarantees attenuation toward the null~\\cite{brenner1993}, the $K{\\geq}3$ case permits $w_{jk}$ to assign negative weight to categories with opposite-sign coefficients, producing \\emph{sign reversal}. A near-permutation confusion matrix effectively swaps category labels, making sign reversal the dominant outcome (83.4\\% under uniform Dirichlet prior). This failure mode is structurally impossible at $K{=}2$ because a $2{\\times}2$ stochastic matrix with positive diagonal cannot reverse the sign of a single coefficient.\n\n\\paragraph{Mediation ($A$ = mediator).}\nWhen $A$ mediates the $X{\\to}Y$ relationship, the direct effect estimator controlling for $A^*$ has probability limit\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mediation}\n\\text{plim}\\;\\hat{\\tau}_{\\text{direct}}(A^*) = \\tau_{\\text{direct}} + \\bigl(1 - \\lambda(C)\\bigr)\\,\\tau_{\\text{indirect}},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\lambda(C) \\in [0,1]$ summarizes attenuation (Appendix~\\ref{app:proof}, Proposition~3), with $\\lambda(I) = 1$ under perfect classification. Misclassification ($\\lambda < 1$) causes the residual indirect effect to be absorbed into the direct path estimate, producing upward bias in $|\\hat{\\tau}_{\\text{direct}}|$ but preserving the sign ($\\tau_{\\text{sign\\_flip}} = 0$).\n\n\\paragraph{Collider ($A$ = collider, conditioned on $A^*$).}\nConditioning on a collider $A$ of $X$ and $Y$ opens a spurious path. When we condition on $A^*$ instead, misclassification attenuates this spurious association because $A^*$ is a noisy proxy for $A$. The collider topology thus exhibits a \\emph{protective} property: annotation error reduces rather than amplifies the bias from collider stratification, with $\\tau_{\\text{sign\\_flip}} = 0$.\n\n\\paragraph{M-bias ($A$ = intermediate collider).}\nIn the M-shaped DAG ($U_1 \\to X$, $U_1 \\to A$, $U_2 \\to A$, $U_2 \\to Y$, $X \\to Y$), adjusting for $A$ introduces a small spurious association via the M-bias path~\\cite{greenland2003}. Misclassifying $A$ weakens this adjustment, reducing the M-bias. This topology is \\emph{immune}: $\\hat{\\tau}(A^*)$ is closer to $\\tau$ than $\\hat{\\tau}(A)$ for all non-trivial confusion matrices, yielding $\\text{ASV} = \\infty$.\n\n\\paragraph{Front-door ($A$ = front-door mediator).}\nIn the front-door DAG ($X \\to A \\to Y$ with unmeasured $U \\to X$, $U \\to Y$), the front-door estimator~\\cite{bellemare_bloem2024} uses $A$ to identify effects despite unmeasured confounding. Misclassification attenuates both $X \\to A$ and $A \\to Y$ relationships, producing moderate bias with $\\tau_{\\text{sign\\_flip}} = 0$.\n\n\\paragraph{Instrumental variable ($A$ = instrument).}\nThe Wald estimator~\\cite{angrist_imbens1995} $\\hat{\\tau}_{\\text{IV}} = \\hat{\\text{cov}}(A^*, Y) / \\hat{\\text{cov}}(A^*, X)$ has $C$ in the denominator. Misclassification can drive first-stage coefficients toward zero or reverse their sign, causing the ratio estimator to diverge or flip. Under random Dirichlet matrices, 52.6\\% produce sign reversal, making IV the most fragile topology.\n\n\\paragraph{Structural sign-flip guarantee.}\n\\begin{proposition}[Sign preservation---analytic results]\n\\label{prop:signflip}\nUnder non-differential misclassification with $C_{kk} > 0$ for all $k$:\n\\emph{(a)}~Confounding preserves sign when $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau) = \\operatorname{sign}(\\tau {+} B_{\\mathrm{OV}})$, where $B_{\\mathrm{OV}} {=} \\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A))/\\operatorname{Var}(T)$ is the omitted-variable bias (Cauchy--Schwarz; Appendix~\\ref{app:confounding}).\n\\emph{(b)}~Collider preserves sign when $|\\tau| > \\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g)\\operatorname{Var}(h)}/\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T{\\mid}A)]$ (Berkson attenuation; Appendix~\\ref{app:collider}).\n\\emph{(c)}~Exposure can produce sign reversal for $K {\\geq} 3$, even with $C_{kk} {>} 0.5$ (counterexample in Appendix~\\ref{app:exposure}).\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\paragraph{Empirical Finding 1} (Mediation, M-bias, front-door).\nSign preservation holds under topology-specific conditions: mediation requires $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}) {=} \\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}})$; front-door requires $\\operatorname{sign}(\\lambda\\alpha_U) {=} \\operatorname{sign}(\\lambda\\alpha_U {+} \\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)))$; M-bias is attenuating for all $C$. Verified across 100{,}000 random matrices with zero violations (Appendix~\\ref{app:conditions}); analytic proofs for general $K$ remain open.\n\nThese conditions involve true DGP parameters, which in practice must be estimated. The ASV framework (§\\ref{sec:asv}) provides a complementary assessment that does not require knowledge of the exact sufficient conditions.\n\n\\subsection{Annotation Sensitivity Values}\n\\label{sec:asv}\n\nWe introduce Annotation Sensitivity Values (ASVs) as a unified sensitivity analysis framework, analogous to E-values for unmeasured confounding~\\cite{vanderweele_ding2017} but designed for annotation-induced bias.\n\n\\paragraph{Parameterization.}\nWe parameterize the confusion matrix as $C(\\delta) = (1{-}\\delta)I + \\delta C_0$, where $C_0$ is a reference confusion matrix (e.g., estimated from a validation set) and $\\delta \\geq 0$ controls the severity of misclassification. At $\\delta{=}0$, classification is perfect; at $\\delta{=}1$, classification matches $C_0$. Non-negativity of $C(\\delta)$ requires $\\delta \\leq \\delta_{\\max} = \\min_j \\frac{1}{1 - (C_0)_{jj}}$. Since $(C_0)_{jj} \\in [0,1]$, we have $\\delta_{\\max} \\geq 1$ for all valid $C_0$, ensuring the full range $[0,1]$ is always feasible; all ASV computations are constrained to $[0, \\delta_{\\max}]$. In practice, $C_0$ is estimated from a finite validation set and subject to sampling uncertainty; we recommend bootstrapping $C_0$ and reporting ASV confidence intervals.\n\n\\paragraph{Three ASV types.}\nWe define three complementary measures: (1)~\\textbf{Magnitude ASV} $\\delta^*_{\\text{mag}} = \\min\\{\\delta : |\\text{plim}\\;\\hat{\\tau}(C(\\delta)) - \\tau| > \\epsilon\\}$---analogous to E-values; (2)~\\textbf{Significance ASV} $\\delta^*_{\\text{sig}} = \\min\\{\\delta : |\\text{plim}\\;\\hat{\\tau}(C(\\delta))| < z_{\\alpha/2} \\cdot SE(N)\\}$---relevant for attenuating topologies; (3)~\\textbf{Sign-flip ASV} $\\delta^*_{\\text{sf}} = \\min\\{\\delta : \\text{sign}(\\hat{\\tau}) \\neq \\text{sign}(\\tau)\\}$---exposure and IV only. The significance ASV depends on $N$ through $SE(N)$, exhibiting a sharp phase transition at a critical $N^*$ below which all rejections are blocked.\n\n\\begin{theorem}[Envelope monotonicity---confounding]\n\\label{thm:monotone}\nFor the confounding topology under $C(\\delta) = (1{-}\\delta)I + \\delta C_0$, $|B(C(\\delta))|$ is monotonically non-decreasing in $\\delta \\in [0, \\delta_{\\max}]$.\n\\end{theorem}\n\n\\emph{Proof sketch.} When $f {=} \\lambda g$ (proportional effects), $B(C(\\delta)) {=} \\lambda V_W(\\delta)/(V_W(\\delta) {+} \\sigma_T^2)$ where $V_W(\\delta) {=} \\operatorname{Var}(g) {-} \\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g{\\mid}A^*_\\delta])$. As $\\delta$ increases, $A^*$ becomes a noisier channel for $A$, so $\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g{\\mid}A^*_\\delta])$ decreases by the data processing inequality, increasing $V_W$. Since $x/(x{+}c)$ is increasing for $c{>}0$, $|B|$ is non-decreasing. The general case follows from Cauchy--Schwarz bounds on $R(C(\\delta))$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:confounding}).\n\n\\begin{conjecture}[Envelope monotonicity---remaining topologies]\n\\label{conj:monotone}\nFor exposure, mediation, front-door, and IV topologies, $|B(C(\\delta))|$ is monotonically non-decreasing in $\\delta$. For collider and M-bias (protective topologies), $|B(C(\\delta))|$ is monotonically non-increasing in $\\delta$: annotation error attenuates the spurious association from conditioning on a collider. Verified numerically across 500 scenarios per topology with zero violations.\n\\end{conjecture}\n\n\\subsection{Binarization Collapse}\n\\label{sec:binarization_method}\n\nCollapsing $K$-class annotations to binary before analysis discards multi-class error structure. We quantify this ``binarization collapse'' as $\\Delta = |\\text{bias}_{K} - \\text{bias}_{K \\to 2}|$.\n",

        "content": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe validate the bias taxonomy and ASV framework through analytical derivations and Monte Carlo simulation across seven DAG topologies under $K{=}3$ non-differential misclassification.\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nFor each of the seven topologies (confounding, exposure, mediation, collider, M-bias, front-door, IV), we sample 100{,}000 confusion matrices from a Dirichlet$(1,1,1)$ prior, compute the analytical probability limit of the causal estimator, and validate against Monte Carlo simulation ($N{=}10{,}000$). ASV experiments use $C(\\delta) = (1{-}\\delta)I + \\delta C_0$.\n\n\\subsection{Bias Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy_results}\n\n\\paragraph{Theory-MC agreement.}\nAnalytical probability limits match Monte Carlo estimates with grand MAPE of 2.0\\% across 126 scenarios (18 LLM configurations $\\times$ 7 topologies, $N{=}10{,}000$). Per-topology MAPE ranges from 0.5\\% (confounding) to 5.5\\% (IV); the IV maximum reflects the Wald estimator's small-denominator variance amplification.\n\n\\paragraph{Exposure DAG: catastrophic sign reversal.}\nUnder the full Dirichlet$(1,1,1)$ prior, 85\\% of $K{=}3$ confusion matrices produce bias that violates the binary ``toward-null'' guarantee. More critically, 83.4\\% of matrices cause \\emph{sign reversal} of at least one coefficient. The two failure modes are distinct: for a coefficient with true value $\\beta_1{=}1.0$, a near-permutation $C$ produces $\\hat{\\gamma}_1{=}{-}0.84$ (sign reversal, magnitude $0.84{\\times}$); for $\\beta_2{=}{-}0.5$, the same $C$ yields $\\hat{\\gamma}_2{=}{-}1.41$ (same-sign amplification, $2.83{\\times}$).\n\nThis finding persists in practically relevant subspaces. When restricting to confusion matrices with diagonal entries $\\geq 0.7$ (typical of current LLM classifiers achieving $F_1 > 0.8$), the violation rate remains 13.7\\% with 2.1\\% sign reversal and 11.7\\% amplification. Even at diagonal $\\geq 0.9$, violation persists at 11.9\\%. In contrast, the $K{=}2$ control confirms that all six tested binary confusion matrices maintain the classical bias-toward-null guarantee~\\cite{brenner1993}, establishing that the multi-class failure is structurally distinct from the binary case.\n\n\\paragraph{Structural guarantee for non-exposure topologies.}\nFive of seven topologies exhibit zero sign-flip rate across all sampled matrices (Table~\\ref{tab:taxonomy}). Confounding, mediation, and front-door show 100\\% violation and amplification but never reverse the sign.\\footnote{Consistent with \\citet{brenner1993} and \\citet{dosemeci1990}: polytomous misclassification can bias \\emph{away} from the null (amplification), but our sign-flip metric confirms the effect sign is preserved.} Collider misclassification attenuates the spurious association (protective). M-bias shows the weakest sensitivity (53.2\\% violation, 10.4\\% amplification) because error on a collider variable reduces the spurious M-bias path.\n\n\\paragraph{IV: Wald estimator instability.}\nThe instrumental variable topology is the sole exception among non-exposure DAGs. The Wald estimator's first stage involves $C$ directly in the denominator, and 52.6\\% of random confusion matrices cause the denominator to change sign, producing $\\tau$-specific sign reversal. This rate remains stable across $K{=}3,5,7$, indicating that IV fragility is a structural property of the Wald estimator rather than a finite-$K$ artifact. Diagonal-dominance subspace analysis (Appendix~\\ref{sec:iv_dirichlet_subspace}) reveals that IV sign reversal vanishes at diagonal $\\geq 0.5$, but unlike exposure, the violation rate remains at 100\\% (pure amplification) even at diagonal $\\geq 0.9$.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-$K$ robustness.}\nAll structural patterns persist at $K{=}5,7$ (100{,}000 matrices each): sign-flip guarantees hold for the same five topologies, and IV sign-flip rates remain within 51--53\\%.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Bias characteristics of $K{=}3$ non-differential misclassification across 7 DAG topologies. Violation: $|\\hat{\\tau}| > |\\tau|$ (bias exceeds true effect); Sign flip: $\\text{sign}(\\hat{\\tau}) \\neq \\text{sign}(\\tau)$; Amplification: $|\\hat{\\tau}| > |\\tau|$ with same sign; $\\tau_{\\text{sf}}{=}0$?: structural sign-flip guarantee holds (\\checkmark) or not (\\ding{55}). Rates over 100{,}000 Dirichlet$(1,1,1)$ random confusion matrices.}\n\\label{tab:taxonomy}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Topology} & \\textbf{Violation} & \\textbf{Sign Flip} & \\textbf{Amplif.} & $\\tau_{\\text{sf}}{=}0$? \\\\\n\\midrule\nConfounding & 100\\% & 0\\% & 100\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nExposure & 85\\% & 83.4\\% & 14.1\\% & \\ding{55} \\\\\nMediation & 100\\% & 0\\% & 100\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nFront-door & 100\\% & 0\\% & 100\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nCollider & 100\\% & 0\\% & 100\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nM-bias & 53.2\\% & 0\\% & 10.4\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nIV & 100\\% & 52.6\\% & 100\\% & \\ding{55} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe bias distributions across topologies are visualized in Figure~\\ref{fig:bias_distribution}, where the exposure panel's dominant red region (sign reversal) contrasts with the symmetric attenuation patterns of other topologies.\n\n\\subsection{ASV Framework Validation}\n\\label{sec:asv_results}\n\n\\paragraph{Envelope monotonicity.}\nThe ASV envelope (bias magnitude as a function of $\\delta$) is monotonically non-decreasing for all seven topologies, with zero violations across all tested scenarios. This validates the theoretical monotonicity property and confirms that $\\delta$ serves as a meaningful ``dial'' controlling annotation error severity.\n\n\\paragraph{Magnitude ASV.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:asv_grid} reports magnitude ASV ranges at $\\tau{=}0.5$ with a 10\\% bias threshold. The ranking from most fragile to most robust is: front-door (0.064--0.387) $<$ mediation (0.076--0.454) $<$ confounding (0.081--0.487) $<$ IV (0.101--0.607) $<$ collider (0.338--2.028) $<$ M-bias ($\\infty$). The collider's high ASV reflects its protective nature: misclassification attenuates the spurious association rather than amplifying it. M-bias achieves infinite ASV because annotation error on the collider variable $A$ only reduces the already-small bias from the M-shaped confounding path.\n\n\\paragraph{Significance and sign-flip ASV.}\nAt $\\tau{=}0.05$, significance ASV achieves 100\\% power for confounding, front-door, and M-bias, but 0\\% for mediation, collider, and IV (correct behavior for attenuating topologies; Type~I error is 0\\%). For sign-flip ASV, 83.4\\% of exposure reference matrices yield $\\tau_{\\text{sign\\_flip}} < 1$ (catastrophic sensitivity); IV sign-flip ASV ranges 1.1--6.7 (moderate-to-high). The complete ranking (Figure~\\ref{fig:asv_ranking}, Table~\\ref{tab:asv_grid}, Appendix~\\ref{app:supplementary}) confirms IV as maximally fragile and M-bias as immune (ASV $= \\infty$).\n\n\\paragraph{$N$-sensitivity.}\nThe significance ASV exhibits a sharp phase transition at a critical sample size $N^* \\approx 6{,}000$--$10{,}000$ (analytically computable per topology), below which all rejections are blocked.\n\n\\subsection{Binarization Collapse}\n\\label{sec:binarization}\n\nCollapsing $K{=}3$ annotations to binary introduces additional bias varying 154$\\times$ across topologies: from $\\bar{\\Delta}{=}0.006$ (M-bias) to $\\bar{\\Delta}{=}0.926$ (IV). Exposure and IV also introduce sign flips during binarization (14\\% and 17.5\\%), a phenomenon absent in the other topologies. Full results are in Appendix~\\ref{app:supplementary} (Figure~\\ref{fig:binarization}).\n\n\\subsection{Empirical Validation}\n\\label{sec:empirical}\n\nWe validate using real LLM confusion matrices from three tasks: HumAID~\\cite{alam2021humaid} ($K{=}10$), VAST ($K{=}3$), and CivilComments ($K{=}2$). Four LLMs (Llama-4-Maverick, Qwen3-235B, DeepSeek-Chat for HumAID; Qwen2.5-7B for VAST/CivilComments) produce 14 configurations under zero-shot and few-shot prompting, yielding 98 bias scenarios ($14 \\times 7$ topologies).\n\n\\paragraph{HumAID ($K{=}10$): theory--practice alignment.}\nThe empirical bias ranking matches predictions: IV (12.4--42.1\\%), exposure (9.0--17.4\\%), mediation/front-door (10.7--14.8\\%), while collider, confounding, and M-bias remain below 10\\%---consistent with the ASV vulnerability spectrum.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-$K$ comparison.}\nBias varies substantially across settings: VAST ($K{=}3$) produces 65.4\\% mean bias with IV reaching 252.6\\%, versus 12.2\\% for HumAID ($K{=}10$) and 37.0\\% for CivilComments ($K{=}2$). Even binary tasks with $F_1{=}0.72$--$0.75$ produce catastrophic IV bias (101--131\\%), confirming that classification accuracy does not predict downstream causal risk (Figure~\\ref{fig:f1_vs_bias}).\\footnote{VAST and CivilComments use a 7B model (vs.\\ frontier for HumAID), and VAST's near-degenerate confusion matrix (third-class recall $=4.6\\%$) may drive its extreme IV bias. These factors limit cross-dataset comparisons but not within-dataset topology-dominance conclusions.}\n\nZero sign reversals occur across all 98 bias scenarios ($14 \\times 7$), confirming the structural sign-flip guarantee is $K$-invariant.\\footnote{Two additional frontier-model configurations for VAST extend this to 112 scenarios across 16 configurations.} This zero rate is consistent with the theoretical prediction: real LLM confusion matrices are diagonally dominant, falling in the safe region of the Dirichlet landscape where sign reversal requires near-random off-diagonal structure. The 7B-to-235B bias ratio is 1.24, indicating topology rather than model scale determines downstream bias.\n\n\\paragraph{ASV diagnostic results.}\nFor HumAID ($K{=}10$, 56 scenarios), 55.4\\% are flagged as magnitude-ASV-unsafe---IV, mediation, front-door, and exposure all trigger, while confounding, collider, and M-bias remain below 10\\% bias (Figure~\\ref{fig:asv_heatmap}). For CivilComments ($K{=}2$), 100\\% of scenarios are ASV-unsafe despite $F_1{=}0.72$--$0.75$.\n\nOverlaying all 18 empirical matrices onto the theoretical Dirichlet violation-rate curves (Figure~\\ref{fig:dirichlet_overlay}, Appendix~\\ref{app:supplementary}) confirms placement: VAST in the danger zone, HumAID mid-range, CivilComments approaching safe for exposure but still in IV amplification.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig5_f1_vs_bias.pdf}\n\\caption{Topology determines downstream causal bias, not $F_1$. HumAID $K{=}10$ (56 points): within $F_1$ range 0.614--0.690, bias varies $10{\\times}$ (IV 42.1\\% vs.\\ collider $<$5.6\\%).}\n\\label{fig:f1_vs_bias}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Correction Guidance}\n\\label{sec:correction}\n\nWe evaluate five correction methods (naive plug-in, MLA, DSL~\\cite{egami_hinck_stewart_wei2023}, MC-SIMEX, PPI++~\\cite{angelopoulos2023}) across all seven topologies under six error structures (210 configurations; full results in Table~\\ref{tab:correction}, Appendix~\\ref{app:supplementary}).\n\n\\paragraph{FWL invariance.}\nFor four topologies where $A$ enters as a covariate (confounding, mediation, collider, M-bias), the Frisch--Waugh--Lovell theorem implies that applying $C^{-1}$ to the dummy variables cannot change the $T$ coefficient. Naive plug-in and MLA achieve exactly 0.0\\% bias reduction across these four topologies.\n\n\\paragraph{Method ranking.}\nMeasuring RMSE reduction ($1 - \\text{RMSE}_{\\text{corr}} / \\text{RMSE}_{\\text{uncorr}}$), PPI++ achieves the highest grand-mean reduction (47.0\\%) but requires gold labels and is catastrophically harmful for M-bias ($-$88.0\\%). MC-SIMEX provides gold-free correction (13.3\\% grand mean, best for IV at 59.4\\%) but harms front-door ($-$62.4\\%). DSL corrects confounding (48.0\\%) and mediation (52.9\\%) but amplifies M-bias ($-$267\\%) and IV ($-$206\\%). No single method dominates: correction effectiveness is itself topology-dependent. Gold-free PPI variants~\\cite{zrnic_candes2024} may relax the gold-label requirement; methods bypassing FWL---such as EM imputation of latent true labels and Bayesian measurement error models---offer potential alternatives for the four FWL-invariant topologies.\n\nEmpirical validation on HumAID ($K{=}10$, 30 MC replicates) confirms topology-dependent method superiority: MC-SIMEX outperforms PPI++ in 4 of 7 topologies and reverses from harmful ($K{=}3$) to beneficial ($K{=}10$). A retrospective case study applying the full framework to \\citet{lee_wood_doughty2024} is in Appendix~\\ref{app:case_study}.\n",
        "numLines": 111,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 111
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/experiments.tex"
  },

        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe presented a systematic characterization of how multi-class annotation errors interact with causal DAG topology, jointly analyzing bias direction, sensitivity, and correction across seven canonical DAG topologies. The classical binary guarantee breaks for $K \\geq 3$: exposure and IV topologies produce sign reversal (83.4\\% and 52.6\\%), while five topologies maintain a structural guarantee. The ASV framework reveals a vulnerability spectrum from IV (maximally fragile) to M-bias (immune), and correction effectiveness is itself topology-dependent, with matrix-based methods provably ineffective for four topologies. Immediate extensions include differential misclassification and correlated multi-LLM error structures.\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur framework assumes non-differential misclassification ($P(A^*|A, Y, \\mathbf{Z}) = P(A^*|A)$), which holds when the LLM has no access to $Y$ but may be violated with outcome-conditional prompting. A stress test (Appendix~\\ref{sec:nondiff_stress}) shows moderate robustness ($\\varepsilon{=}0.05$ increases violations by $<$3pp). Closed-form expressions are derived for $K{=}3$ and verified at $K{=}5,7$; arbitrary $K$ remains open. The framework assumes a single annotator; correlated multi-annotator settings~\\cite{kim_garg2025} are future work. VAST and CivilComments use a 7B model rather than frontier models, limiting cross-tier comparisons. All derivations assume linear structural equations; the FWL invariance result does not hold for logistic regression, and bias patterns under nonlinear models may differ.\n",
        "numLines": 9,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 9
      }
    },

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01B5yDgq5P6FZnkMQsGEeQ1X`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Large language models are rapidly replacing human annotators in computational social science, with researchers using LLM-generated labels for tasks ranging from sentiment classification to political stance detection~\cite{egami_hinck_stewart_wei2023, baumann2025}. Crucially, most of these tasks are inherently multi-class: stance detection uses 3--4 categories, sentiment analysis 5, and disaster response taxonomies 10 or more. This shift promises dramatic cost reduction, but introduces a critical validity gap: annotation errors from imperfect classifiers propagate into downstream causal estimates in ways that depend on both the error structure and the causal design. \citet{baumann2025} demonstrate that across 37 annotation tasks, 31\% of hypotheses yield incorrect conclusions with state-of-the-art LLMs, rising to 50\% for smaller models; these errors are highly correlated across models (60\% agreement when both err)~\cite{kim_garg2025}, undermining ensemble-based mitigation. Analyzing the \emph{structure} of annotation error, rather than simply reducing its magnitude, is essential for valid downstream inference.
5	
6	The consequences can be catastrophic. Under the full Dirichlet$(1,1,1)$ prior over $K{=}3$ non-differential exposure misclassification, 83.4\% of confusion matrices cause \emph{sign reversal} of the estimated treatment effect. Under realistic classifier accuracy (diagonal $\geq 0.7$), the violation rate is 13.7\% with 2.1\% sign reversal. This failure breaks the classical binary guarantee that non-differential misclassification biases estimates ``toward the null''~\cite{brenner1993}, a guarantee that many applied researchers implicitly rely upon. Moreover, standard classification metrics do not predict downstream causal risk: within a narrow $F_1$ range of 0.614--0.690 across three frontier LLMs, relative bias varies by an order of magnitude across topologies (from 4.1\% to 42.1\%), illustrating that \emph{topology}, not annotator quality, determines downstream risk.
7	
8	The mechanism is algebraic. For binary classification, non-differential error can only attenuate the effect because the $2{\times}2$ stochastic confusion matrix preserves coefficient signs. With $K \geq 3$ categories, the confusion matrix can assign negative effective weights to categories with opposite-sign coefficients, enabling sign reversal~\cite{veirod_laake2001}. This fundamental asymmetry has received limited systematic treatment. \citet{brenner1993} identified the possibility of polytomous reversal but did not characterize it across causal designs. \citet{lee_wood_doughty2024} extended classifier error analysis to categorical variables and LLM classifiers, but covered only one DAG topology (unobserved confounding). No existing framework addresses how the same confusion matrix produces qualitatively different bias across different causal identification strategies.
9	
10	We address these gaps with three contributions:
11	
12	\begin{enumerate}
13	\item \textbf{Multi-class bias taxonomy.} We derive bias expressions for seven canonical DAG topologies (Figure~\ref{fig:dag_taxonomy}) under $K$-class non-differential misclassification and establish that five topologies maintain a structural sign-flip guarantee while exposure and IV can produce sign reversal. Binarization amplifies the gap: collapsing $K{=}3$ to binary introduces additional bias varying 154$\times$ across topologies.
14	
15	\item \textbf{Annotation Sensitivity Values (ASVs).} We propose three complementary sensitivity measures---magnitude, significance, and sign-flip ASV---revealing a vulnerability spectrum from IV (maximally fragile, 52.6\% sign reversal) to M-bias (immune, ASV $= \infty$).
16	
17	\item \textbf{Topology-aware correction guidance.} We evaluate five correction methods and show that matrix-based corrections achieve exactly 0\% bias reduction for four topologies due to Frisch--Waugh--Lovell invariance. PPI++ achieves 47.0\% RMSE reduction but requires gold labels and is harmful for M-bias ($-$88\%).
18	\end{enumerate}
19	
```

      "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nLarge language models are rapidly replacing human annotators in computational social science, with researchers using LLM-generated labels for tasks ranging from sentiment classification to political stance detection~\\cite{egami_hinck_stewart_wei2023, baumann2025}. Crucially, most of these tasks are inherently multi-class: stance detection uses 3--4 categories, sentiment analysis 5, and disaster response taxonomies 10 or more. This shift promises dramatic cost reduction, but introduces a critical validity gap: annotation errors from imperfect classifiers propagate into downstream causal estimates in ways that depend on both the error structure and the causal design. \\citet{baumann2025} demonstrate that across 37 annotation tasks, 31\\% of hypotheses yield incorrect conclusions with state-of-the-art LLMs, rising to 50\\% for smaller models; these errors are highly correlated across models (60\\% agreement when both err)~\\cite{kim_garg2025}, undermining ensemble-based mitigation. Analyzing the \\emph{structure} of annotation error, rather than simply reducing its magnitude, is essential for valid downstream inference.\n\nThe consequences can be catastrophic. Under the full Dirichlet$(1,1,1)$ prior over $K{=}3$ non-differential exposure misclassification, 83.4\\% of confusion matrices cause \\emph{sign reversal} of the estimated treatment effect. Under realistic classifier accuracy (diagonal $\\geq 0.7$), the violation rate is 13.7\\% with 2.1\\% sign reversal. This failure breaks the classical binary guarantee that non-differential misclassification biases estimates ``toward the null''~\\cite{brenner1993}, a guarantee that many applied researchers implicitly rely upon. Moreover, standard classification metrics do not predict downstream causal risk: within a narrow $F_1$ range of 0.614--0.690 across three frontier LLMs, relative bias varies by an order of magnitude across topologies (from 4.1\\% to 42.1\\%), illustrating that \\emph{topology}, not annotator quality, determines downstream risk.\n\nThe mechanism is algebraic. For binary classification, non-differential error can only attenuate the effect because the $2{\\times}2$ stochastic confusion matrix preserves coefficient signs. With $K \\geq 3$ categories, the confusion matrix can assign negative effective weights to categories with opposite-sign coefficients, enabling sign reversal~\\cite{veirod_laake2001}. This fundamental asymmetry has received limited systematic treatment. \\citet{brenner1993} identified the possibility of polytomous reversal but did not characterize it across causal designs. \\citet{lee_wood_doughty2024} extended classifier error analysis to categorical variables and LLM classifiers, but covered only one DAG topology (unobserved confounding). No existing framework addresses how the same confusion matrix produces qualitatively different bias across different causal identification strategies.\n\nWe address these gaps with three contributions:\n\n\\begin{enumerate}\n\\item \\textbf{Multi-class bias taxonomy.} We derive bias expressions for seven canonical DAG topologies (Figure~\\ref{fig:dag_taxonomy}) under $K$-class non-differential misclassification and establish that five topologies maintain a structural sign-flip guarantee while exposure and IV can produce sign reversal. Binarization amplifies the gap: collapsing $K{=}3$ to binary introduces additional bias varying 154$\\times$ across topologies.\n\n\\item \\textbf{Annotation Sensitivity Values (ASVs).} We propose three complementary sensitivity measures---magnitude, significance, and sign-flip ASV---revealing a vulnerability spectrum from IV (maximally fragile, 52.6\\% sign reversal) to M-bias (immune, ASV $= \\infty$).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Topology-aware correction guidance.} We evaluate five correction methods and show that matrix-based corrections achieve exactly 0\\% bias reduction for four topologies due to Frisch--Waugh--Lovell invariance. PPI++ achieves 47.0\\% RMSE reduction but requires gold labels and is harmful for M-bias ($-$88\\%).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",

  "new_string": "The mechanism is algebraic, and the transition at $K {\\geq} 3$ is a qualitative break, not a gradual degradation. For $K{=}2$, the stochastic constraint forces any confusion matrix with $C_{kk} {>} 0.5$ to preserve coefficient signs---an algebraic property of the $2{\\times}2$ matrix dimension, not of a specific error rate. This guarantee \\emph{vanishes} at $K {\\geq} 3$: the higher-dimensional confusion matrix admits negative effective weights on categories with opposite-sign coefficients, enabling sign reversal even when every diagonal entry exceeds $0.5$~\\cite{veirod_laake2001}."
}

With $K \\geq 3$ categories, the confusion matrix can assign negative effective weights to categories with opposite-sign coefficients, enabling sign reversal~\\cite{veirod_laake2001}.",
    "newString": "The mechanism is algebraic, and the transition at $K {\\geq} 3$ is a qualitative break, not a gradual degradation. For $K{=}2$, the stochastic constraint forces any confusion matrix with $C_{kk} {>} 0.5$ to preserve coefficient signs---an algebraic property of the $2{\\times}2$ matrix dimension, not of a specific error rate. This guarantee \\emph{vanishes} at $K {\\geq} 3$: the higher-dimensional confusion matrix admits negative effective weights on categories with opposite-sign coefficients, enabling sign reversal even when every diagonal entry exceeds $0.5$~\\cite{veirod_laake2001}.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nLarge language models are rapidly replacing human annotators in computational social science, with researchers using LLM-generated labels for tasks ranging from sentiment classification to political stance detection~\\cite{egami_hinck_stewart_wei2023, baumann2025}. Crucially, most of these tasks are inherently multi-class: stance detection uses 3--4 categories, sentiment analysis 5, and disaster response taxonomies 10 or more. This shift promises dramatic cost reduction, but introduces a critical validity gap: annotation errors from imperfect classifiers propagate into downstream causal estimates in ways that depend on both the error structure and the causal design. \\citet{baumann2025} demonstrate that across 37 annotation tasks, 31\\% of hypotheses yield incorrect conclusions with state-of-the-art LLMs, rising to 50\\% for smaller models; these errors are highly correlated across models (60\\% agreement when both err)~\\cite{kim_garg2025}, undermining ensemble-based mitigation. Analyzing the \\emph{structure} of annotation error, rather than simply reducing its magnitude, is essential for valid downstream inference.\n\nThe consequences can be catastrophic. Under the full Dirichlet$(1,1,1)$ prior over $K{=}3$ non-differential exposure misclassification, 83.4\\% of confusion matrices cause \\emph{sign reversal} of the estimated treatment effect. Under realistic classifier accuracy (diagonal $\\geq 0.7$), the violation rate is 13.7\\% with 2.1\\% sign reversal. This failure breaks the classical binary guarantee that non-differential misclassification biases estimates ``toward the null''~\\cite{brenner1993}, a guarantee that many applied researchers implicitly rely upon. Moreover, standard classification metrics do not predict downstream causal risk: within a narrow $F_1$ range of 0.614--0.690 across three frontier LLMs, relative bias varies by an order of magnitude across topologies (from 4.1\\% to 42.1\\%), illustrating that \\emph{topology}, not annotator quality, determines downstream risk.\n\nThe mechanism is algebraic. For binary classification, non-differential error can only attenuate the effect because the $2{\\times}2$ stochastic confusion matrix preserves coefficient signs. With $K \\geq 3$ categories, the confusion matrix can assign negative effective weights to categories with opposite-sign coefficients, enabling sign reversal~\\cite{veirod_laake2001}. This fundamental asymmetry has received limited systematic treatment. \\citet{brenner1993} identified the possibility of polytomous reversal but did not characterize it across causal designs. \\citet{lee_wood_doughty2024} extended classifier error analysis to categorical variables and LLM classifiers, but covered only one DAG topology (unobserved confounding). No existing framework addresses how the same confusion matrix produces qualitatively different bias across different causal identification strategies.\n\nWe address these gaps with three contributions:\n\n\\begin{enumerate}\n\\item \\textbf{Multi-class bias taxonomy.} We derive bias expressions for seven canonical DAG topologies (Figure~\\ref{fig:dag_taxonomy}) under $K$-class non-differential misclassification and establish that five topologies maintain a structural sign-flip guarantee while exposure and IV can produce sign reversal. Binarization amplifies the gap: collapsing $K{=}3$ to binary introduces additional bias varying 154$\\times$ across topologies.\n\n\\item \\textbf{Annotation Sensitivity Values (ASVs).} We propose three complementary sensitivity measures---magnitude, significance, and sign-flip ASV---revealing a vulnerability spectrum from IV (maximally fragile, 52.6\\% sign reversal) to M-bias (immune, ASV $= \\infty$).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Topology-aware correction guidance.} We evaluate five correction methods and show that matrix-based corrections achieve exactly 0\\% bias reduction for four topologies due to Frisch--Waugh--Lovell invariance. PPI++ achieves 47.0\\% RMSE reduction but requires gold labels and is harmful for M-bias ($-$88\\%).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",

beta=[0, 1.0, -0.5], sigma_Y=1.0\\n\\n## plan_001 完整 Taxonomy 结果 (2026-05-20)\\n\\n### 结构性保证：τ sign_flip = 0% 跨所有拓扑和所有 τ 大小\\n**这是论文 Claim 1 的核心 positive result**。K=3 非差异性误分类改变 treatment effect 幅度但不翻转方向。小 τ (0.05, 0.1) 确认是结构性的。Exposure DAG 是唯一例外（83.4% sign reversal）。\\n\\n### 拓扑行为分层\\n- **Mediation/Front-door**: violation ≈100%, amplification ≈100%, misclass_amplifies ≈100% — 最受影响\\n- **Collider**: misclass_amplifies = 0% — 误分类减弱 collider bias（独特）\\n- **M-bias**: violation 53.2%, amplification 10.4% — 最鲁棒\\n- **Exposure**: sign_flip 83.4% — 唯一翻转拓扑（C 直接作用于 design matrix）\\n\\n## plan_002 K=5,7 扩展结果 (2026-05-20)\\n\\n### Sign_flip 随 K 恶化（核心发现）\\n**K 增大引入更多错分模式，符号反转概率系统性上升**：\\n- Mediation: 83.8%(K=3) → 96.6%(K=5) → **100%(K=7)**\\n- Collider: 66.6%(K=3) → 95.5%(K=5) → **99.2%(K=7)**\\n- Confounding/Exposure: K=3 已近饱和，K=5,7 达 100%\\n\\n### 但 worst-case bound 和 bias 均值反而下降（除 exposure）\\n**反直觉的 nuance**：高精度约束(diag≥0.9)下，K 增大每个 off-diagonal 元素分摊更小 → confounding worst-case 从 0.162(K=3) 降至 0.092(K=7)。**唯一例外是 exposure**（0.253→0.282），因为 exposure 的 bias 来自 design matrix 的交叉污染，不受对角占优约束的同样保护。\\n\\n### Eigendecomposition：bias 方差随 K 分散\\nTop-1 PC 解释 bias 方差的比例：78%(K=3) → 67%(K=5) → 61%(K=7)。K 增大后 bias 不再集中在单一方向，增加了诊断和校正难度。\\n\\n### 对论文 story 的影响\\nClaim 1 证据链完整：plan_001(K=3 taxonomy) + plan_002(K scaling) + plan_008(binarization collapse)。可 claim \\\"K 增大使符号反转风险恶化，practitioners 处理高 K 分类任务时风险更大\\\"。\\n\\n## plan_008 Binarization Collapse 结果 (2026-05-20)\\n\\n### 拓扑依赖性：折叠偏差跨度 150 倍\\nIV (Δ_mean=0.926) > Exposure (0.516) >> Mediation (0.045) > Confounding (0.025) > Collider (0.017) > M-bias (0.006)。Sign flip 只在 IV (17.5%) 和 Exposure (14.0%)。plan_001 和 plan_008 的拓扑危险性排序一致。\\n\\n## plan_004 ASV 框架结果 (2026-05-20)\\n\\n### v2 发现 + v3 修正\\nv2 问题：(1) 单调性 75% 违反 (2) sign-flip ASV 对 confounding/mediation 返回 None\\n\\nv3 修正（D007 + Director magnitude 阈值修正）：\\n- **Envelope monotonicity**: violations = 0（构造性保证）\\n- **Magnitude ASV (10% relative threshold)**: 对所有拓扑有信息\\n  - τ=0.5: 0.076–0.487（部分 C₀ 下安全）\\n  - τ=0.1: 0.014–0.093（不安全）\\n  - τ=0.05: 0.008–0.047（极不安全）\\n- **关键洞察**：小 τ ASV << 1 = LLM 标注误差对小效应因果推断不安全 → 连接 Claim 3 correction 需求\\n- **Type I/II (小 τ)**: ASV-informed 正确保守（H₁ 下 0% 拒绝，因为 ASV<1 → 标记不安全）\\n\\n### ASV 三类定义（最终版）\\n1. **Sign-flip ASV**: min δ 使 sign 翻转（Exposure 专用，其他拓扑 = None）\\n2. **Magnitude ASV**: min δ 使 |bias| > 0.1×|τ̂|（通用，所有拓扑有信息）\\n3. **Significance ASV**: min δ 使 |plim τ̂(C(δ))| < 1.96×SE（假设检验 filter）\\n\\n## plan_005 ASV 扩展结果 (2026-05-20, fixed)\\n\\n### 6 拓扑 ASV 全覆盖（Reviewer CRITICAL 修复后）\\n修复 4 点：C1 significance ASV 函数 + C2 magnitude τ≈0→0.0 + W1 IV τ_true=plim(I)=0.6598 + W2 M-bias τ_true=plim(I)=0.5128\\n\\n### τ_true 必须用 plim(I) 而非 DGP 参数\\n**设计决策**：ASV 衡量 annotation error **额外引入**的 bias，不含估计量固有的多类别污染。因此 τ_true = plim(C=I)，不是 DGP 直接参数。IV plim(I)=0.6598≠β₁=0.5；M-bias plim(I)=0.5128≠τ=0.5。Front-door 已正确。\\n\\n### Significance filter Type I/II（修复后）\\n| 拓扑 | Type I | Power τ=0.05 | Power τ=0.1 |\\n|------|--------|-------------|-------------|\\n| M-bias | 0% | 95.8-99.2% | 100% |\\n| IV | 0% | 0% | 0% |\\n| Front-door | 0% | 100% | 100% |\\n\\n- **IV power=0% 是正确的**：Wald 估计量极脆弱，signif_ASV≈0（annotation error 立即摧毁 significance），ASV filter 保守阻止所有拒绝。对 IV，应改用 magnitude ASV 报告脆弱性\\n- **M-bias 高 power**：误分类减弱 spurious bias，不影响真实效应的检测\\n- 与 plan_004 win rate 95.2% (40/42 scenarios)。唯一满足名义 95% coverage 的方法。\\n\\n### Frisch-Waugh 不变性：理论到实证完全验证\\nnaive_plugin 和 MLA 在 OLS 拓扑（confounding/mediation/collider/mbias）下 bias_red 精确 0.0%。原因：C⁻¹ 变换 dummy matrix 不改变 [intercept, D*] 的列空间，T 系数 OLS 估计不变。\\n但在 exposure/iv/frontdoor 下两者显著恶化（-40% ~ -113%），因为这些拓扑的估计量不是简单 OLS on T → FWL 不适用。\\n\\n### DSL 拓扑敏感性（新发现，可作为 contribution）\\nDSL 仅在 confounding (49.8%) 和 mediation (70.3%) 有效——这两种拓扑 misclassification error 主要通过 dummy 系数传播，DSL 的 EM 迭代能捕获。其余 5 拓扑系统性失败，最差 mbias -768%。**失败原因是结构性的，非个别 C 问题。**\\n\\n### PPI++ 的阿喀琉斯之踵\\nmbias + realistic_llm: bias_red = -25.1%（恶化），但 coverage 99%。说明 PPI 的置信区间正确反映了增大的不确定性，bias 校正本身失效。论文应诚实报告此弱点。\\n\\n### MC-SIMEX 不稳定\\nGrand mean 20.9%，在 iv (64.7%) 尚可但 frontdoor (-55.7%) 崩溃。不适合作为通用推荐。\\n\\n## plan_006 ASV 实证校准 — humaid ASV 仅触发 mediation/exposure/IV/frontdoor，与理论预测吻合\\n- ✅ 0 sign flips — K=10 真实 LLM C 对角占优仍保护符号\\n\\n### Inter-model error independence\\nError correlation 0.05-0.16（低），Kappa 需求\\\\n- **Type I/II (小 τ)**: ASV-informed 正确保守（H₁ 下 0% 拒绝，因为 ASV<1 → 标记不安全）\\\\n\\\\n### ASV 三类定义（最终版）\\\\n1. **Sign-flip ASV**: min δ 使 sign 翻转（Exposure 专用，其他拓扑 = None）\\\\n2. **Magnitude ASV**: min δ 使 |bias| > 0.1×|τ̂|（通用，所有拓扑有信息）\\\\n3. **Significance ASV**: min δ 使 |plim τ̂(C(δ))| < 1.96×SE（假设检验 filter）\\\\n\\\\n## plan_005 ASV 扩展结果 (2026-05-20, fixed)\\\\n\\\\n### 6 拓扑 ASV 全覆盖（Reviewer CRITICAL 修复后）\\\\n修复 4 点：C1 significance ASV 函数 + C2 magnitude τ≈0→0.0 + W1 IV τ_true=plim(I)=0.6598 + W2 M-bias τ_true=plim(I)=0.5128\\\\n\\\\n### τ_true 必须用 plim(I) 而非 DGP 参数\\\\n**设计决策**：ASV 衡量 annotation error **额外引入**的 bias，不含估计量固有的多类别污染。因此 τ_true = plim(C=I)，不是 DGP 直接参数。IV plim(I)=0.6598≠β₁=0.5；M-bias plim(I)=0.5128≠τ=0.5。Front-door 已正确。\\\\n\\\\n### Significance filter Type I/II（修复后）\\\\n| 拓扑 | Type I | Power τ=0.05 | Power τ=0.1 |\\\\n|------|--------|-------------|-------------|\\\\n| M-bias | 0% | 95.8-99.2% | 100% |\\\\n| IV | 0% | 0% | 0% |\\\\n| Front-door | 0% | 100% | 100% |\\\\n\\\\n- **IV power=0% 是正确的**：Wald 估计量极脆弱，signif_ASV≈0（annotation error 立即摧毁 significance），ASV filter 保守阻止所有拒绝。对 IV，应改用 magnitude ASV 报告脆弱性\\\\n- **M-bias 高 power**：误分类减弱 spurious bias，不影响真实效应的检测\\\\n- 与 plan_004 confounding/mediation/collider 结果合并 → 6 拓扑 significance filter 完整验证\\\\n\\\\n## plan_007 Correction Guidance 结果 (2026-05-20)\\\\n\\\\n### PPI++ 全面碾压（Claim 3 核心支撑）\\\\nGrand mean: bias_red 93.8%, coverage 96.8%, win rate 95.2% (40/42 scenarios)。唯一满足名义 95% coverage 的方法。\\\\n\\\\n### Frisch-Waugh 不变性：理论到实证完全验证\\\\nnaive_plugin 和 MLA 在 OLS 拓扑（confounding/mediation/collider/mbias）下 bias_red 精确 0.0%。原因：C⁻¹ 变换 dummy matrix 不改变 [intercept, D*] 的列空间，T 系数 OLS 估计不变。\\\\n但在 exposure/iv/frontdoor 下两者显著恶化（-40% ~ -113%），因为这些拓扑的估计量不是简单 OLS on T → FWL 不适用。\\\\n\\\\n### DSL 拓扑敏感性（新发现，可作为 contribution）\\\\nDSL 仅在 confounding (49.8%) 和 mediation (70.3%) 有效——这两种拓扑 misclassification error 主要通过 dummy 系数传播，DSL 的 EM 迭代能捕获。其余 5 拓扑系统性失败，最差 mbias -768%。**失败原因是结构性的，非个别 C 问题。**\\\\n\\\\n### PPI++ 的阿喀琉斯之踵\\\\nmbias + realistic_llm: bias_red = -25.1%（恶化），但 coverage 99%。说明 PPI 的置信区间正确反映了增大的不确定性，bias 校正本身失效。论文应诚实报告此弱点。\\\\n\\\\n### MC-SIMEX 不稳定\\\\nGrand mean 20.9%，在 iv (64.7%) 尚可但 frontdoor (-55.7%) 崩溃。不适合作为通用推荐。\\\\n\\\\n## plan_006 ASV 实证校准 — humaid 先行结果 (2026-05-20)\\\\n\\\\n### 真实 LLM confusion matrices (K=10, HumAID)\\\\n- deepseek 最好（mean_diag 0.724），qwen3 次之（0.710），llama4 最差（0.678）\\\\n- Class 6 (\\\\\\\"other_relevant_information\\\\\\\") 是所有模型的主要混淆目标\\\\n- Macro F1: 0.614–0.690（全部 <0.93，K=10 高难度任务）\\\\n\\\\n### 理论-实证一致性验证\\\\n- ✅ IV 最脆弱（12-42% bias）— 与 plan_005 signif_ASV≈0 一致\\\\n- ✅ Collider 最稳健（4-6% bias）— 与 plan_001 misclass_amplifies=0% 一致\\\\n- ✅ Magnitude ASV 仅触发 mediation/exposure/IV/frontdoor，与理论预测吻合\\\\n- ✅ 0 sign flips — K=10 真实 LLM C 对角占优仍保护符号\\\\n\\\\n### Inter-model error independence\\\\nError correlation 0.05-0.16（低），Kappa 0.39-0.49（fair-moderate）。模型犯错较独立 → 对 PPI++ 有利。\\\\n\\\\n### ASV filter 过于保守\\\\nASV-unsafe 49.2%，但 unsafe 时 power 直接归零。实际应有梯度式衰减——这是方法论改进方向。\\\\n\\\\n### 待补充\\\\n需 vast (K=3) + civil_comments (K=2) 数据验证 Baumann claim (F1>0.93 仍有风险) 和 K 值对比。\\\\n\\\\n## plan_009 Pathological Matrices 结果 (2026-05-20)\\\\n\\\\n### 3 风险层级\\\\n- **Low risk**: mbias (mean_bias 0.006-0.039)\\\\n- **Medium risk**: confounding/mediation/collider/frontdoor (R²=0.70-0.77, condition_number 最一致预测因子)\\\\n- **High risk**: exposure/IV (不可预测，R²低)\\\\n\\\\n### IV 过滤问题\\\\n3000/28000 (10.7%) non-finite Wald estimates 被静默排除。regression 仅基于 89.3% 的样本。\\\\n\\\\n## plan_010 annot_sensitivity Toolkit (2026-05-20)\\\\n\\\\n### 设计决策\\\\n- **Bias 计算用 precomputed sufficient stats + plim**（2M 样本），不跑 MC——秒级响应，非分钟级\\\\n- **IV 拓扑用 Wald estimator**（W1 修复 2026-05-21）：`RF / (C @ FS)[1]`，near-permutation C → -904.8% bias + sign flip\\\\n- **recommend_correction 编码 plan_007 全部结论**：has_gold → PPI++; confounding/mediation → DSL; 其余 → MC-SIMEX; naive_plugin/MLA 标注 FWL=0%\\\\n- **K 不硬编码**：API 接受任意 K 的 C 矩阵，precomputed stats 按 (topology, params, K) 缓存\\\\n- **τ_true = plim(C=I)**：与 plan_005 一致，衡量 annotation error 额外引入的 bias\\\\n- **condition number 警告**：C condition > 1e12 时 warn，> 100 时在 recommend_correction 中提示\\\\n- **测试**: 47/47 passed（含 IV Wald 4 tests + correction 8 tests）\\\\n\\\\n## Local Model Inference (2026-05-21)\\\\n\\\\n### RTX 5090 + Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct 本地推理\\\\n- **vLLM 与 RTX 5090 (Blackwell sm_120) 不兼容**（flash_attn 不支持），改用 transformers + model.generate() (batch_size=8)\\\\n- annotate.py 添加 `--local-model` 参数 + `LocalAnnotationPipeline` 类\\\\n- model_key = \\\\\\\"qwen2.5-7b\\\\\\\"，model 字段为 \\\\\\\"qwen2.5-7b-local\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n### 标注结果（civil_comments K=2 + vast K=3）\\\\n| Dataset | Prompt | Accuracy | Diag Avg |\\\\n|---|---|---|---|\\\\n| civil_comments | zero-shot | 74.2% | 0.717 |\\\\n| civil_comments | few-shot-3 | 68.9% | 0.730 |\\\\n| civil_comments | few-shot-5 | 73.9% | 0.749 |\\\\n| vast | zero-shot | 48.0% | 0.473 |\\\\n| vast | few-shot-3 | 46.1% | 0.453 |\\\\n| vast | few-shot-5 | 46.5% | 0.459 |\\\\n\\\\n- civil_comments: 质量可用（69-74%），7B 模型对 toxicity 判断合理\\\\n- vast: 质量较低（46-48%），7B 模型对 stance detection 困难——但正好提供 \\\\\\\"7B vs 235B 对比\\\\\\\" 维度\\\\n- 全部 parse rate ≥ 99.9%\\\\n\\\\n## Director 决策记录 (2026-05-20)\\\\n\\\\n### 数据集决策（最终确认）\\\\n- (2026-05-20)\\n\\n### Exposure DAG: bias-toward-null 保证在 K=3 下失效（核心发现）\\n**100k 随机 C 搜索：85% 违反 bias-toward-null，83.4% 产生符号反转。** 二分类 K=2 下全部满足 toward null 保证。\\n\\n具体 named structure 结果：\\n- C3_STRUCTURAL_12: β_2=-0.5 → γ̂_2=+0.06（符号反转）\\n- C4_WEAK_DIAG: β_1=1.0 → γ̂_1≈0（近消失）\\n- C5_NEAR_PERM: β_2=-0.5 → γ̂_2=+1.25（符号反转 + 2.5x 放大，最极端）\\n\\n**关键洞察**：exposure 误分类场景下，多分类 C 矩阵的交叉污染使得回归将某类的 Y 值归因到错误标签，产生符号反转。\\n\\n### Dirichlet 子空间分析：高精度 LLM 仍有显著违反\\n即使 min(diag(C))≥0.9，amplification 违反率仍达 11.9%。符号反转随精度提高消失，但 amplification 稳定在 ~12%。\\n\\n### Confounding DAG: 偏差方向性质\\n100k 随机 C 搜索均未发现负偏差。K=3 误分类偏差始终与 OVB 同向。\\n\\n### DGP 参数\\n- Confounding DAG: P(A)=[0.4, 0.35, 0.25], alpha=[1.5, -1.0], beta_T=2.0, beta_A=[1.0, -0.5], sigma=1.0\\n- Exposure DAG: P(A)=[0.4, 0.35, 0.25], beta=[0, 1.0, -0.5], sigma_Y=1.0\\n\\n## plan_001 完整 Taxonomy 结果 (2026-05-20)\\n\\n### 结构性保证：τ sign_flip = 0% 跨所有拓扑和所有 τ 大小\\n**这是论文 Claim 1 的核心 positive result**。K=3 非差异性误分类改变 treatment effect 幅度但不翻转方向。小 τ (0.05, 0.1) 确认是结构性的。Exposure DAG 是唯一例外（83.4% sign reversal）。\\n\\n### 拓扑行为分层\\n- **Mediation/Front-door**: violation ≈100%, amplification ≈100%, misclass_amplifies ≈100% — 最受影响\\n- plan_002 K=5,7 扩展结果 (2026-05-20)\\n\\n### Sign_flip 随 K 恶化（核心发现）\\n**K 增大引入更多错分模式，符号反转概率系统性上升**：\\n- Mediation: 83.8%(K=3) → 96.6%(K=5) → **100%(K=7)**\\n- Collider: 66.6%(K=3) → 95.5%(K=5) → **99.2%(K=7)**\\n- Confounding/Exposure: K=3 已近饱和，K=5,7 达 100%\\n\\n### 但 worst-case bound 和 bias 均值反而下降（除 exposure）\\n**反直觉的 nuance**：高精度约束(diag≥0.9)下，K 增大每个 off-diagonal 元素分摊更小 → confounding 方差的比例：78%(K=3) → 67%(K=5) → 61%(K=7)。K 增大后 bias 不再集中在单一方向，增加了诊断和校正难度。\\n\\n### 对论文 story 的影响\\nClaim 1 证据链完整：plan_001(K=3 taxonomy) + plan_002(K scaling) + plan_008(binarization collapse)。可 claim MC-SIMEX; naive_plugin/MLA 标注 FWL=0%\\n- **K 不硬编码**：API 接受任意 K 的 C 矩阵，precomputed stats 按 (topology, params, K) 缓存\\n- **τ_true = plim(C=I)**：与 plan_005 一致，衡量 annotation error 额外引入的 bias\\n- **condition number 警告**：C condition > 1e12 时 warn，> 100 时在 recommend_correction 中提示\\n- **测试**: 47/47 passed（含 IV Wald 4 tests + correction 8 tests）\\n\\n## Local Model Inference (2026-05-21)\\n\\n### RTX 5090 + Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct 本地推理\\n- **vLLM 与 RTX 5090 (Blackwell sm_120) 不兼容**（flash_attn 不支持），改用 transformers + model.generate() (batch_size=8)\\n- annotate.py 添加 `--local-model` 参数 + `LocalAnnotationPipeline` 类\\n- model_key = \\\"qwen2.5-7b\\\"，model 字段为 \\\"qwen2.5-7b-local\\\"\\n\\n### 标注结果（civil_comments K=2 + vast K=3）\\n| Dataset | Prompt | Accuracy | Diag Avg |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| civil_comments | zero-shot | 74.2% | 0.717 |\\n| civil_comments | few-shot-3 | 68.9% | 0.730 |\\n| civil_comments | few-shot-5 | 73.9% | 0.749 |\\n| vast | zero-shot | 48.0% | 0.473 |\\n| vast | few-shot-3 | 46.1% | 0.453 |\\n| vast | few-shot-5 | 46.5% | 0.459 |\\n\\n- civil_comments: 质量可用（69-74%），7B 模型对 toxicity 判断合理\\n- vast: 质量较低（46-48%），7B 模型对 stance detection 困难——但正好提供 \\\"7B vs 235B 对比\\\" 维度\\n- 全部 parse rate ≥ 99.9%\\n\\n## Director 决策记录 (2026-05-20)\\n\\n### 数据集决策（最终确认）\\n- HumAID (K=10), VAST (K=3), Civil Comments (K=2)\\n- 数据位置：`/home/sonny/causal_annot_error_attrib/results/`（注意是 results/ 不是 data/）\\n\\n### API 模型决策\\n- Llama 4 Maverick, Qwen 3 235B, DeepSeek Chat V3\\n- API endpoint: `http://47.94.22.126/v1`，全部用 OPENROUTER_API_KEY\\n- **2026-05-20 API 402 事故**：运行中余额耗尽，~10/27 任务完成，civil_comments 全废\\n\\n### home-1x5090 服务器\\n- account: sonny, Python 3.10.12, Ubuntu 22.04\\n- 项目目录 `/home/sonny/causal_annot_error_attrib/`\\n\\n### ASV 框架决策 (D005-D008)\\n- D005: C(δ) 参数化选定方案 A 线性插值\\n- D006: 区分 Vulnerability/Protection 双方向 ASV\\n- D007: Envelope monotonicity 替代 raw monotonicity\\n- D008: ASV 阈值从 sign-flip 改为 magnitude-based（0.1×|τ̂|），sign-flip ASV 保留给 Exposure\\n\\n### Lee & Wood-Doughty (2024) 技术区分\\n本项目：observed exposure/confounder with noisy LLM annotation。LWD：unobserved confounding with proxy。关键区别：我们的 C 可从 validation data 估计。\\n\\n## ASV C(δ) 参数化调研 (2026-05-20)\\n\\n### 关键文献\\n- VanderWeele & Ding (2017): E-value 框架——单标量参数化 + 单调 + 可解释\\n- VanderWeele & Li (2019): 测量误差灵敏度分析——Se/Sp ratio 参数化\\n- Lash, Fox & Fink (2009): QBA 框架——(Se, Sp) 对参数化\\n- Brenner (1993): 多分类混杂误分类偏差方向可反转\\n\\n### 与 E-value 的对应\\n方案 A 在 K=2 时精确退化到 VanderWeele & Li
